# Experiment logs

One log per bring-up experiment. The logs are ordered chronologically, and the
numbering is offset from the stage numbering: **experiment NN corresponds to stage
NN-3**. Experiment 01 and 02 predate the `stageN/` directory convention, so they
have no stage.

Path commands inside these logs are a record of what was actually run at the time.
For the six retained snapshots the paths have been updated to the current layout
(`stages/stage86/...`); the logs belonging to the archived stages (00-84) keep
their original commands, since those stages are no longer in the working tree -
see `../../stages/README.md` for the retention policy and how to restore an
archived stage with `tools/stage-archive.sh`.

Safety held: `fastboot boot` only, nothing flashed, `persistent_write_attempted=0x00000000` ×25, `failure_mask=0x00000000` ×87, zero aborts, and the device came back to Android — with the honesty gap that the checkpoint run's log was overwritten before its counters were read. | stage90 | [experiment-281](experiment-281-the-pad-and-the-first-silence-that-was-the-instruments.md) | **the pad, and the first silence that turned out to be the instrument's** — the bisect 280's silence forced, and its first product: XNU's own `bcopy_phys` calls at `cpu.c:570-580` write `BootArgs_paddr` and `CpuDataEntries_paddr` to `gPhysBase + (&ResetHandlerData.boot_args - &ExceptionLowVectorsBase)`, which Apple's own link resolves into the vectors blob at the image base and *this* link resolves to **0x80002408 and 0x80002404 — inside `entry_epilogue` at `0x80002348`**, the function that writes every log line this walk has read. The two instructions destroyed were `way_shift = 32u - n` and `way = 0`; the values written are addresses, which decode as register-form `ANDS` with `cond=HI` that set neither register, so the way loop counts from whatever `lr` held and the epilogue never reaches its ram-console write — **a hang inside the reporting path looks exactly like a hang in the boot**. The fix is on this side: a NOP pad placed *first* in the sweep block, wider than the eight bytes at risk. Also the `AFTER` variant of the checkpoint instrument (`STAGE90_ENTRY_CHECKPOINT_AFTER=1`: call the real function, record `cp_ret`, report after it — so `cp_ret=<v>` means it returned and a silence means it did not), which composes with `SKIP` and whose one limitation is measured, not argued: the wrapper's frame sits where a callee reads its *stack* arguments, so `bcopy_phys` is not an `AFTER` candidate while `bcopy` is. Runs: `pmap_map_cpu_windows_copy` silent (one call site, not reached); `mmu_kvtop_wpreflight` reports `0x80002408`/`0x80000000`/`0x80800000`; the same with `AFTER` reports `cp_ret=0x80002408` — the preflight returns; `bcopy SKIP=2 AFTER` reports `cp_calls=3` with args `0x80300000`/`0x80304000`/`0x4000` and caller `arm_vm_init+0xb0`, **refuting the call census while naming itself through its arguments**. 25 x `persistent_write_attempted=0x00000000`, 87 x `failure_mask=0x00000000`, zero aborts |

| stage90 | [experiment-282](experiment-282-r7-and-the-four-reports-that-cleared-the-instrument.md) | **`r7`, and the four reports that showed the silence was the instrument's** — the pad was only half a fix, and the missing half was the report path *itself*. Three versions: no pad (lost `way_shift`/`way`, so the sweep hangs); **a pad of NOPs** ("a NOP overwritten by an `ands` is still a NOP" — false whenever something *executes* it, which the report always does); a run-time repair of the NOPs with `dsb`/`isb`, built, verified in the linked image and **still silent** because the store lands in the D-cache while the fetch of the pad's line may already have been prefetched — an instrument whose correctness rests on an unmeasurable cache property is not an instrument. The weapon, measured in the linked disassembly: `entry_epilogue` loads **`r7` from `[entry_vectors_stack + 4]` at `0x80002358` and does not touch it again until `0x80002574`**, so `0x80147000` decoding as `andshi r7, r4, r0` (register-form `ANDS`, `cond=HI`, so it writes `r7` exactly when C is set and Z clear) destroys a live value whose later uses are a kv word, half a pointer and a byte-table base. The fix is structural: the pad now opens with **`b 1f` over 31 NOPs and is never executed**, so XNU's writes land on NOPs nothing fetches as instructions, whatever the caches and flags do; `build_entry.sh` checks that *structure* against two linked labels (`entry_skip_pad` branches to `entry_skip_pad_end` over ≥128 bytes, itself ≥4 bytes below `0x80002404`, with both written addresses strictly inside what it skips). Then three reports: `CleanPoC_DcacheRegion` (caller `0x80004310`, args `0x80000000`/`0x00001000`) proves **both `bcopy_phys` calls returned** — so 282m's silence was never "the stop is inside `bcopy_phys`"; `machine_startup` (caller `0x8000334c`, `cp_arg0=0x80147000`) proves the whole of `arm_init`'s ladder ran; and a plain run — the walk's normal mode, unavailable for four experiments — reports `stub_hit=kernel_set_special_port`, the symbol the 280 era predicted from reading `ipc_host.c`. The walk bisected a four-byte `memmove` that returns, with an instrument that was the thing that was wrong: **this project's "a measurement can be the thing that is wrong" defect for the eighth time**. 25/87 clean on every run |

This is the step 413's prediction was for: same name, same key, one run later, with the guarded site that pre-empted it now retired. **Two readings made it, and 413 had only one of them.** First, *both* lists of the walk for every function on the path (defect 128 fixed at the point of use): the only stubs they name are the four `mac_cred_label_*` this step retires. Second, **the `zalloc` path, which is new territory** — `mac_cred_label_init` → `mac_cred_label_alloc` → `mac_labelzone_alloc` → `zalloc` is the first real `zalloc` this boot would run: `MAC_WAITOK` is defined **0** (`security/mac_policy.h:6960`), so the `tst r1,#1 / bne` at `mac_labelzone_alloc+0x18` falls through to `zalloc`, and the tool's guarded chain names `OSBacktrace`, `btlog_add_entry` and `trace_backtrace` as stubs "1 guard further". **Those were closed by a source reading, not a walk** — they sit behind `__improbable(DO_LOGGING(zone))` and the `#if ZONE_DEBUG` block, and a zone's `zlog_btlog` is NULL unless `Z_LOGGING` is set, which `mac_label.c:43` does not set (only `Z_EXPAND`, `Z_EXHAUST`, `Z_CALLERACCT`). And the MAC machinery is bounded by there being **no policy loaded**: `mac_policy_init` sets `mac_policy_list.numloaded = 0` and nothing here is a kext, so no `mpo_*` callback is reachable. Every call target of all seventeen resolved names was checked against the stub list (`mac_labelzone_alloc`, `mac_policy_list_conditional_busy`, `mac_policy_list_unbusy`, `mac_error_select`, `mac_externalize`, `posix_cred_get`, `bcmp` — all real). | stage90 | [experiment-438](experiment-438-the-option-headers-carry-their-component-and-the-stop-becomes-a-pseudo-inits-entry-read-out-of-a-stub-body.md) | **the option headers carry their *component*, two stubs retire at once, and the stop becomes an instruction prefetch abort at an address that *is* an instruction** — `tools/gen_option_headers.py` now writes `out/xnu_options/<CONFIG>/<component>/meta_features.h` beside the flat one, `build_xnu_arm_kernel.sh` puts a component's own directory **ahead of** the flat header for every manifest file that belongs to it, and the new `tools/check_option_headers.py` — run by the build — makes both halves structural. **The cause 437 named was wrong and the measurement is two files away.** 437 said `kmstartup` (`bsd_init + 0x844`) was stopped by `bsd/kern/subr_prof.c` using the macro `STATIC`. It is stopped by `bsd_init.c:852` being `#ifdef GPROF` while **`libkern/conf/files:5` is `OPTIONS/gprof optional gprof`** — so the single flat `meta_features.h` defined `GPROF 0` for every translation unit, and `#define GPROF 0` is not *off* to `#ifdef`, it is *defined*. A BSD file called a profiler entry point, whose only definer is `subr_prof.c` — and that file's body is inside the same `#ifdef` and does not compile, because `STATIC` is *also* undefined there. The two facts are one fact. **The fix is a faithful reproduction of a checked piece of Apple's build, not a fudge:** `mkheaders.c:71-79` walks *the file table it is given*, `makedefs/MakeInc.dir` runs the config tool over each component's own `conf/files`, and `MakeInc.def:466` is `INCFLAGS_LOCAL = -I.` — the component's own object directory. Which options a translation unit sees is a property of the **component**. The comment in `scan_options()` asserted the opposite (*"the value is the same whichever component declared it"*) — the *value* is, the *membership* is not. **Membership alone is not a rule either, and the scan is what says so: exactly two cross-component reads exist in the whole tree.** `GPROF` (libkern → bsd) is **not shared** — every one of those blocks is dead when the option is off, `nm` over all 695 objects finds no reference to `mcount`, `_gmonparam` or `cfreemem`, and it is the only reading under which Apple's own `subr_prof.c` compiles; `CONFIG_MACF` (bsd+security → osfmk) **is shared**, because `osfmk/kern/task.h:241` guards a `struct task` field with `#ifdef` while `task.c` guards the same parameter with `#if` — narrowing it would give `struct task` two layouts in one kernel, an ABI mismatch no compiler can see. The check keys on *declarers*, not on first-declarer: `MACH_ASSERT` is declared by osfmk, bsd **and** iokit, so a bsd file reading it reads its own (found by exactly that mistake on `bsd/kern/kern_credential.c`), and its `KNOWN_READS` table is validated in both directions so it cannot rot. **Prediction: check passes both configs, 695 objects → 696, 3 failures → 2, `U kmstartup` gone, every other object byte-identical, stubs 44 → 43, and the run returns from `bsd_init` into `kernel_bootstrap_thread`. Falsifiers: a stop still at `kmstartup`, a profiler-era `data abort`, `bsd_init` still not returning, a `struct task` of two sizes.** **Measured: no falsifier fired, and three predictions were wrong.** Pool **695 → 696, 3 → 2 failures**, `bsd_kern_subr_prof.o` new — and the byte comparison is the load-bearing half: **exactly two objects changed, `bsd_kern_bsd_init.o` and `bsd_kern_subr_xxx.o`, the other 693 byte-identical**, including `bsd_kern_kern_clock.o`, the one the prediction called identical. **Miss (a): 44 → 42 stubs, because `subr_prof.c` is not the profiler.** Its `#ifdef GPROF` is 83–339 and **`addupc_task` at line 370 is outside it**; `resourcevar.h:124` expands it unguarded and `kern_clock.c:379`/`kern_sig.c:3346` call it, so it *was* a stub, and `T addupc_task 0x8028bb7c` is now real. Two stubs retire from two opposite directions — `kmstartup` because its **caller** stopped existing, `addupc_task` because its **definer** started existing. *A file's name is not its `#ifdef` structure.* **Miss (b): `.text` grew `0x4B4AC0 → 0x4B4B00`, +0x40, and changed nothing else** — image still 5141584, `.data` `0x804B8000`/`0x2E360`, `.sysctl_set` `0x804E6360`/`0xFD8`, `.init_array` `0x804E7338`/`0x118`, bss `0x804E7480..0x805388F8` all unmoved, the copied image still 48 bytes below `__bss_start` — **and the entry bin's hash moved anyway** (sha256 `2e735451…`): equal size is not equal layout. Closure 423 → 424 objects; `copyin`/`copyout` cost nothing because the image already defines both. **Miss (c), the run: the stop is not in `kernel_bootstrap_thread`.** The result line has **no `stub_hit=` for the first time**, and a fault block 436 and 437 never printed: `exception: prefetch abort`, `ifar=0xE52DE004`, `ifsr=0x00000005` (translation fault, section), `lr=0xE52DE008`, `spsr=0xA0000013`, `ttbr0=ttbr1=0x8070404A`, `abort_entries=0`, `checks=5`/`failures=0`, `kv_dropped=0`. `kv_written 0x226 → 0x346` — **+0x120 = 9 × 32 bytes and nothing else moved**, because `fleh_prefabt` writes its records only when it runs: 437 printed *nothing* for those keys, not zeros, so **no stub was hit at all**. **`0xE52DE004` is the ARM encoding of `push {lr}`**, and both `IFAR` and `LR_abt` agree on it, so the processor really was fetching from that address. It occurs in the image as a code word in exactly 43 places: **42 are the `push {lr}` of the 42 stub bodies** (laid out in the order of `xnu_arm_entry_undef.txt`, 0x18 bytes apart — a second, independent measurement of the stub count) and the 43rd is `inv_shift_rows`' first instruction. Every stub is `movw r0,#name; push {lr}; mov r1,lr; movt r0,#name; pop {lr}; b entry_stub_hit`. **And that is the whole stop, measured end to end:** `bsd_init.c:861` is `bsd_autoconf();` — inside `bsd_init` itself — and `bsd_autoconf` is `for (pi = pseudo_inits; pi->ps_func; pi++) (*pi->ps_func)(pi->ps_count);` over `struct pseudo_init { int ps_count; int (*ps_func)(int); }`, which `bsd/dev/busvar.h:46` declares **`extern struct pseudo_init pseudo_inits[]`**. The linked loop is `movw/movt r4,#0x80444e3c; ldr r1,[r4,#4]; beq exit; ldr r0,[r4]; blx r1`, and at `0x80444e3c` — `pseudo_inits`, entry **28** of the alphabetical undefined list — the two words are `0xE3020928` (`movw r0`) and **`0xE52DE004`** (`push {lr}`). **`pseudo_inits` is a *function* stub where the header says the symbol is an *array of structs***: the `0` that ends the walk is the array's first `ps_func`, and our stand-in put its own prologue there, so `blx r1` at `bsd_autoconf+0x28` jumps to it and the fault follows. `tools/xnu_entry_callwalk.py --root bsd_autoconf` reports *"no stub on the straight-line path … indirect calls the walk could not follow: `bsd_autoconf+0x28: blx r1`"* — **the walk's blindness and the device's fault are the same instruction.** **A third face of the symptom, and the first of its kind on hardware: the stop is a stand-in of the wrong *kind*.** The undefined list shows names and never kinds, so nothing host-side could have said the symbol was declared an array. **Safety: 25 × `persistent_write_attempted=0x00000000`, 87 × `failure_mask=0x00000000`, `abort_entries=0`, no `panic:` line, `kv_dropped=0`**, hardware watchdog the only net across the jump (`bite_truncated=0`, `timeout_s=25`), dead-man disarmed before it, `fastboot boot` only, nothing flashed, 302532 bytes / 3994 lines ending `No errors detected`, device back on Android on its own (`cancro`, release 10). **Next: supply `pseudo_inits` as the array the header declares** — `mkioconf.c:79-100` says what it contains (one `{count, func}` per `PSEUDO_DEVICE` with a `d_init`, terminated by `{0,0}`), so it is *generated* from the device table, and the check that must stop the build is a **kind** check: a symbol declared an array must not be stubbed as a function. |

So the design is one section descriptor into XNU's live L1, memory type Strongly-ordered, with four refusals before the write and the console's signature read back after it (1 no neighbour, 2 slot in use, 3 domain fault, 4 no signature, 5 no uncached encoding), and the descriptor's protection bits *inherited* from a neighbour section — XNU's own BLOCK for `RAM_CONSOLE_BASE - 1 MB`. **The neighbour does not exist.** The 451 comment asserted `boot_args.memSize` is `0x5e500000`; that is `xmaxmem`, the clamp `arm_vm_init` applies, and the log the same run produced says `xnu_entry_args_memSize=0x01000000` in its own ladder, three thousand lines above the live records that were never written. `_start`'s `mapveqp` walks `memSize` / 1 MB = **16** sections and `invalidate_tte` faults the whole 16 KB boot table first, so the live L1 holds indices `0x800..0x80f` and `0xfff` and nothing else: index `0xde4` is fault, the 4096-entry scan found nothing, and it refused with 1 — into `.bss`, where nothing reads it. The state record answers why none of it was visible: `opsidx=0x0` (the **serial** console, switched by `initialize_screen`'s no-video branch, `video_console.c:2846-2852`, from `PE_create_console` at `arm_init.c:365`) and `kputc=0x8009b1a4` = **`cnputc`**, so `serial_init()` failed, `uart_initted` is 0 and **`uart_putc` is a function that does nothing** — the console was wired to the one sink that cannot print, while the video sink's `gc_initialized` gate is never set either; `noserial=1` (kprintf off), `noconout=0`. **The copyright banner was never printed**: `bsd_init`'s `printf(copyright)` is `mov r0,#0; bl _consume_printf_args` in the image — `CONFIG_NO_PRINTF_STRINGS` (`config/MASTER:302`, `bsd/libkern/libkern.h:171-173`, `osfmk/kern/misc_protos.h:150-152`) makes `printf` a no-op in `bsd/kern` and most of BSD/OSFMK, while `pexpert/arm/pe_init.c` includes neither header and calls the real one — so `cannot mount root, errno = %d`, `We are hanging here...` and `bsd_init: failed to mount network root` **will never print** until the build changes, while every `IOLog` will. Frontier unmoved: three `iolock` records at `waitForMatchingService+0xE0` (site `0x801365d4`, symbol `0x801364f4`), `deadline − now` 29.9966/29.9936/59.9943 s, `finddrv` 2, `rootdrv` 1, 38 `block_seq`; `IOLog("Still waiting for root device\n")` (`:559`) is absent, so the watchdog returned the device at ~25 s inside the boot's own 60 s deadline. That the first fault was answered and not merely reported is what the OS console says: it gains one line over 466 and it is two frames into the function the fault was in — `load_init_program: failed loading /sbin/launchd: errno 8` (`kern_exec.c:5169`), after `load_init_program_at_path`'s three `copyout`s and its `execve`; the tracer's own `_dropped` goes 33017 -> **33079**, 62 more recorded events than 466 reached, and `stub_hit_count=0` says none of them was a stand-in. **The stop is XNU's own named panic, one level below the fault.** `panic_entered=1`, `panic_len=0x1295` with `_dropped=0`, and the trap is the predicted `udf`: `undef_pc=0x800355ac` in `DebuggerTrapWithState` (`800355ac: e7ffdefe udf #65006`), `trap_r9_fmt=0x804a0766` = `"Process 1 exec of %s failed, errno %d"`, `panic_arg0=0x804a0919` = `"/sbin/launchd"`, `_arg1=8`. **`errno 8` is `ENOEXEC` and it is not the malformed-Mach-O answer** (`load_return_to_errno` maps `LOAD_BADMACHO` to `EBADMACHO`, `kern_exec.c:5203`): it is "Image not claimed by any activator" (`kern_exec.c:2803-2805`), because all three activators decline a file whose first four bytes are zero — `exec_mach_imgact` (`:855`), `exec_fat_imgact` (`:643`), `exec_shell_imgact` (`:477`, `:482`), each returning `-1`. All three build counts exact (755/668/87); `__bss_end` unchanged at 0x80167798 for the **second step running** and by the same mechanism (the object's 0x28 plus fill grows `realstubs.o`'s `.bss` *start* by 0x40, the retired `machine_info` stand-in shrinks its *size* by 0x40 — start + size unmoved), image unchanged at 1247700. **`.text` 0x117120 → 0x1176C0, 0x128 below the estimate, and the whole decomposition is measurable in the map**: `machine.o` `.text` +0x5F0, its `.rodata.str1.1` **+0x3A against 0xC7 in the object** (the map prints `0x3a … (size before relaxing) 0xc7`), `realstubs.o` `.text` −0x60 (**five** stubs retire, not one), its `.rodata.str1.4` −0x3C, alignment +0x12 = +0x5A0 to the byte. **A `.rodata.str*` size in an object is an upper bound, not a contribution** — a third kind of number in this project's list, after the total read as a component (298, 300) and the component read as a total. 25 x `persistent_write_attempted=0x00000000`, 87 x `failure_mask=0x00000000`, `xnu_entry_failures=0x00000000`, no `exception:` line, device back on its own (release 10, cancro) |

| stage90 | [experiment-298](experiment-298-the-orphan-sections-get-names-and-the-sysctl-set-gets-a-segment.md) | **The orphan sections get names, and `__DATA,__sysctl_set` gets a segment** — the six Mach-O input-section names the objects carry become named output sections: `*("__TEXT,*")` and `.ARM.exidx` into `.text`, `*("__DATA,*__data" "__DATA,*__const")` into `.data`, and `__DATA,__sysctl_set` into an output section of its own placed between `.data` and `.bss`, plus a `verify_sections` check that closes the set. **The quoting is load-bearing and was tested with a two-object link first** — a pattern containing a space or a comma must be quoted, and a quoted pattern still takes wildcards, so one entry covers both of XNU's spellings. Two of the six placements were wrong, and neither was the memset's business: `__TEXT, initcode` is *code* (`memorystatus_init`) and sat inside `__DATA`; and `__DATA,__sysctl_set` is read **by name through the Mach-O header** (`LINKER_SET_BEGIN(__sysctl_set)` → `getsectdatafromheader(_header, "__DATA", "__sysctl_set", &size)`), so it has to be inside the `__DATA` **segment**, where as an orphan it was not. The set is *not* merged into `.data` — its name is its interface, and a merged section has no address a Mach-O entry could describe. **A correction to 297, from the input sections' flags:** ld's placement is consistent, not arbitrary (every `A`-only section with the read-only group, every `WA` one after `.data`) — so the lesson is that the layout contract depended on a decision it had not made, not that ld is unpredictable. Every line held except one: `.text` +0x9C0, `.bss`/`__bss_start` +0xC0, `.sysctl_set` 0x80130930/0xa4, image +164, all exactly; and the `size` column's "text" is not `.text` (it is `.text` plus every read-only section outside it — the 2648-byte orphan group before, `.sysctl_set`'s 164 now), so that one predicted `+0x9C0` and measured `+12`. **`stub_hit=kpc_thread_create` at `xnu_entry_stub_caller=0x8000b034` — unchanged, which is the proof that nothing that executes moved.** 25 x `persistent_write_attempted=0x00000000`, 87 x `failure_mask=0x00000000`, no `exception:` line, device back on its own |

| stage90 | [experiment-297](experiment-297-bss-start-is-the-start-of-bss-and-the-3252-bytes-that-were-erased.md) | **`__bss_start` is the start of `.bss`, and the 3252 bytes every run was erasing** — one line moves, from between the `.data` and `.bss` output sections to the first statement *inside* `.bss`. It was assigned between them, so it named the end of `.data`; it names the start of `.bss` only if the linker places nothing in the gap, and the linker put the two Mach-O orphans there (`"__DATA, __const"`, `"__DATA, __data"` — the **space** in those names is why `*(.data .data.*)` misses them). The payload memsets `[__bss_start, __bss_end)` *after* copying the image in, so **every run this project has ever made zeroed 3252 bytes of initialized data**: 122 `vm_allocation_site`s, all with `refcount = 2`, **129 non-zero bytes** in the file — 117 plain `kalloc` sites, 3 `kalloc_tag`, 2 `kalloc_tag_bt`, and the 5 tagged ones carry the `tag` that `vm_tag_alloc_locked`'s `if (site->tag) return;` reads. This is the project's own answer, taken from the other linker script: `stages/stage90/linker.ld:19` has always put the assignment *inside* `.bss`, so the two scripts had disagreed for as long as both existed and **the entry one was the wrong one**. Everything predicted held, and the **image is byte-identical** — the section table is unchanged address for address, so the only difference in what the device is handed is the one constant the payload compiles in. `__bss_start` 0x8012fc78 → **0x80130940**; `xnu_entry_bss_bytes` 0x37aa0 → **0x36dd8**; the copy ends at 0x80130930, so the two ranges are disjoint by 16 bytes. **The stop does not move** — `stub_hit=kpc_thread_create` at `xnu_entry_stub_caller=0x8000b034` — so the state is right and the boot has not yet reached the place where it mattered. Two checks: `verify_bss` (structural) and **`bss_start - ENTRY_BASE >= bin_size`** (arithmetic over the payload's own numbers — and the check that would have failed the 296 build, 0xCC8 short). 25 x `persistent_write_attempted=0x00000000`, 87 x `failure_mask=0x00000000`, no `exception:` line, device back on its own |
